Among the first papers where supercurrents were defined and discussed
we mention, e.g., works
Ferrara:1974pz
;
Ogievetsky:1976qc
.
.

There are two essentially different types of the current supermultiplets:

•

Symmetry current supermultiplet (for example, multiplets involving currents of internal symmetry);

•

Supercurrent (it contains a fermionic current generating rigid supersymmetry together with the stress tensor associated with space-time translations).

The structure of
\mathcal{N}=1
current superfields is well known, see, e.g.,
Dumitrescu:2011zz
;
Bertolini
. The construction of higher spin conserved

\mathcal{N}=1
current superfields was presented
in refs.
Howe:1981qj
;
HK1
;
Buchbinder:2018wzq
;
Buchbinder:2018nkp
;
Buchbinder:2018gle
;
Gates:2019cnl
;
Hutomo:2020wca
. We shall describe in Section
C.1

\mathcal{N}=2
harmonic superfields that accommodate various current supermultiplets. In particular, we shall present harmonic superfields containing higher spin currents.

The structure of
\mathcal{N}=1
supercurrents is also well known, see for example
BK
;
Kuzenko:2010am
;
Bertolini
. In
\mathcal{N}=2
theories the structure of supercurrents
was elaborated in
Butter:2010sc
. However, to the best of our knowledge, the supercurrent structure in harmonic superspace was not systematically discussed so far.

C.1
\mathcal{N}=2
current superfields

1.
We consider the real (with respect to the tilde conjugation,
\widetilde{J^{++A}}=J^{++A}
) analytic superfield
J^{++A}
,
where
A
is some multi-index of the form
A=\alpha(n)\dot{\alpha}(n)
.
We impose the “harmonic conservation law” on the superfield
J^{++A}

22
22

22

This conservation law formally coincides with the harmonic constraint
\mathcal{D}^{++}L^{++}=0

defining
\mathcal{N}=2
tensor multiplet
L^{++}(\zeta)
off shell. For details see Section 6.2 of
18
.
:

\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{++A}=\partial^{++}J^{++A}-2i\theta^{+\alpha}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}J^{++A}=0\,.

(C.1)

Here we assumed that the supercurrent does not depend on
x^{5}
.

The general solution of the constraint
\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{++A}=0
is given by:

\begin{split}J^{++A}(x,\theta^{+},u)=&j^{(ij)A}(x)u^{+}_{i}u^{+}_{j}+\theta^{+\alpha}j^{iA}_{\alpha}(x)u^{+}_{i}+\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\alpha}}\bar{j}^{iA}_{\dot{\alpha}}(x)u^{+}_{i}\\
&+(\theta^{+})^{2}j^{A}(x)+(\bar{\theta}^{+})^{2}\bar{j}^{A}(x)\\
&+2i\theta^{+}\sigma^{n}\bar{\theta}^{+}\left[j_{n}^{A}(x)+\partial_{n}j^{(ij)A}(x)u^{+}_{i}u^{-}_{j}\right]\\
&-i(\bar{\theta}^{+})^{2}\theta^{+\alpha}\partial^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\alpha}\bar{j}^{iA}_{\dot{\alpha}}u^{-}_{i}-i(\theta^{+})^{2}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\dot{\alpha}}^{\alpha}j^{iA}_{\alpha}u^{-}_{i}\\
&-(\theta^{+})^{2}(\bar{\theta}^{+})^{2}\Box j^{(ij)A}u^{-}_{i}u^{-}_{j}\,.\end{split}

(C.2)

As independent components, it involves the real field
j^{(ij)A}(x)
, the complex field
j^{iA}_{\alpha}(x)
and its complex conjugate
\bar{j}^{iA}_{\dot{\alpha}}(x)
, the complex field
j^{A}(x)
and
the real field
j_{n}^{A}(x)
. Equation (
C.1
) implies the current conservation condition for the latter:

\partial^{n}j_{n}^{A}=0\,.

(C.3)

This allows us to identify the harmonic superfield
J^{++A}
with the supermultiplet containing the conserved current and the equation
\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{++A}=0
with the relevant superfield conservation condition.
All bosonic higher spin current superfields considered in Section
5
have such a structure.

2.
Fermionic higher spin current superfields are also analytic, but, in contrast to bosonic higher spin current superfields, they satisfy different conditions (
5.55
):

\begin{cases}\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{+}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}=-2i\theta^{+\rho}J^{++}_{(\alpha(s-2)\rho)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}-2i\bar{\theta}^{+}_{(\dot{\alpha}}J^{++}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2))}\,,\\
\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{++}_{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}=0\,,\\
\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{++}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}=0\,.\end{cases}

(C.4)

Using the general solution (
C.2
) of the second and third equations in (
C.4
) and substituting it in the first equation o,
one can obtain, for the coefficient in the
\theta^{+\rho}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\rho}}
piece of the fermionic current superfield,

J^{+}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}=\dots+2i\theta^{+\rho}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\rho}}j^{i}_{\rho\dot{\rho}\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}u^{-}_{i}+\dots\,,

(C.5)

the following conservation condition:

\partial^{\rho\dot{\rho}}\left(j^{i}_{\rho\dot{\rho}\alpha(s-2)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\sigma})}-j^{i}_{\dot{\rho}(\alpha(s-2)\rho)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\sigma})}+\epsilon_{(\dot{\sigma}\dot{\rho}}j^{i}_{\rho\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2))}\right)=0\,.

(C.6)

Here, the fields
j^{i}_{\dot{\rho}\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}
and
j^{i}_{\rho\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}
are the appropriate fermionic components of the bosonic current superfields

J^{++}_{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}
and
J^{++}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}
, respectively.

So we conclude that the fermionic current, defined as

\mathfrak{j}^{i}_{{\rho\dot{\rho}\alpha(s-2)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\sigma})}}:=j^{i}_{\rho\dot{\rho}\alpha(s-2)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\sigma})}-j^{i}_{\dot{\rho}(\alpha(s-2)\rho)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\sigma})}-\epsilon_{\dot{\rho}(\dot{\sigma}}j^{i}_{\rho\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2))}

(C.7)

is conserved as a consequence of the superfield equations (
C.4
).

3.
An analogous consideration for the complex conjugate current superfield (
5.62
),

\begin{cases}\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{+}_{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}=2i\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\rho}}J^{++}_{\alpha(s-1)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\rho})}-2i\theta^{+}_{(\alpha}J^{++}_{\alpha(s-2))\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\,,\\
\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{++}_{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}=0\,,\\
\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{++}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}=0\,,\end{cases}

(C.8)

evidently implies that the corresponding fermionic component,

J^{+}_{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}=\dots+2i\theta^{+\rho}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\rho}}j^{i}_{\rho\dot{\rho}\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}+\dots\,,

(C.9)

obeys the conservation law:

\partial^{\rho\dot{\rho}}\left(j^{i}_{\rho\dot{\rho}(\alpha(s-2)\sigma)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}-j^{i}_{\rho(\alpha(s-2)\sigma)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\rho})}+\epsilon_{\rho(\sigma}j^{i}_{\dot{\rho}\alpha(s-2))\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\right)=0\,.

(C.10)

Here, the fields
j^{i}_{\rho\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}
and
j^{i}_{\dot{\rho}\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}
are proper
fermionic components of the bosonic current superfields

J^{++}_{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}
and
J^{++}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}
, respectively.
Thus the relevant conserved fermionic current is defined by:

\mathfrak{j}^{i}_{\rho\dot{\rho}(\alpha(s-2)\sigma)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}:=j^{i}_{\rho\dot{\rho}(\alpha(s-2)\sigma)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}-j^{i}_{\rho(\alpha(s-2)\sigma)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\rho})}+\epsilon_{\rho(\sigma}j^{i}_{\dot{\rho}\alpha(s-2))\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\,.

(C.11)

In Sections
5.6
and
5.8
we presented the explicit expressions of these current superfields in terms of the free hypermultiplet
in the spin
\mathbf{2}
(see also Appendix
C.2
) and the spin
\mathbf{3}
cases.

In Appendix
D
the conservation conditions (
C.1
), (
C.4
) and (
C.8
) will be independently derived
by making use of
\mathcal{N}=2
superfield Noether theorem for rigid symmetries of the free
hypermultiplet.

C.2
\mathcal{N}=2
supercurrent

Let us now consider the special supercurrent
J^{++\alpha\dot{\alpha}}
having an additional conservation law:

\begin{cases}\mathcal{D}^{++}J^{++\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=0\,,\\
\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}J^{++\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=0\,.\end{cases}

(C.12)

The second condition implies the conservation of all the component currents in its relevant
\theta
-expansion (
C.2
):

\begin{cases}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}j^{(ij)\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=0\,,\\
\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}j^{i\alpha\dot{\alpha}}_{\beta}=0\,,\\
\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}j^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=0\,,\\
\partial_{m}j_{n}^{m}=0\,.\end{cases}

(C.13)

These currents can be identified with the currents inherent to any
\mathcal{N}=2
super Poincaré invariant theory:
j_{n}^{m}
with the energy-momentum tensor,

j^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}
with
{\rm U}(1)
R-symmetry current,
j^{i\alpha\dot{\alpha}}_{\beta}
with the doublet of supersymmetry fermionic currents and
j^{(ij)\alpha\dot{\alpha}}
with the
{\rm SU}(2)
R-symmetry current.

In what follows we will use the notation:

\begin{split}J^{++}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}(x,\theta^{+},u)=&R^{(ij)}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}(x)u^{+}_{i}u^{+}_{j}+\theta^{+\beta}S^{i}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta}(x)u^{+}_{i}+\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\beta}}\bar{S}^{i}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}(x)u^{+}_{i}\\
&+(\theta^{+})^{2}R_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}(x)+(\bar{\theta}^{+})^{2}\bar{R}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}(x)\\
&+2i\theta^{+}\sigma^{n}\bar{\theta}^{+}\left[T_{n\,\alpha\dot{\alpha}}(x)+\partial_{n}R^{(ij)}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}(x)u^{+}_{i}u^{-}_{j}\right]\\
&-i(\bar{\theta}^{+})^{2}\theta^{+\beta}\partial^{\dot{\beta}}_{\beta}\bar{S}^{i}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}u^{-}_{i}-i(\theta^{+})^{2}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\beta}}\partial_{\dot{\beta}}^{\beta}S^{i}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta}u^{-}_{i}\\
&-(\theta^{+})^{2}(\bar{\theta}^{+})^{2}\Box R^{(ij)}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}u^{-}_{i}u^{-}_{j}\,.\end{split}

(C.14)

For example, the analytic current superfield (
5.81
) constructed out of the hypermultiplet superfield
q^{+}
, has such a structure
and so can be identified with the
\mathcal{N}=2
supercurrent superfield of hypermultiplet, as was already mentioned in Section
5.6
.

Appendix D
\mathcal{N}=2
Noether theorem and rigid symmetries of hypermultiplet

In this Appendix, we briefly review the issue of higher-derivative rigid symmetries of the free hypermultiplet action defined in
Buchbinder:2022kzl

and relate these symmetries to the conserved current superfields.

D.1
Rigid symmetries

The rigid symmetries of hypermultiplet considered in
Buchbinder:2022kzl
are realized by the following transformations
23
23

23

Here
s\geq 2
.
For
s=1
, the symmetry transformation is:

\delta_{rig}^{(s=1)}q^{+a}=-\lambda Jq^{+a}.

Unlike these transformations, all higher spin (
s\geq 2
) transformations contain derivatives.
:

\delta^{(s)}_{rig}q^{+a}=-\hat{\Lambda}_{rig}^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+a}\,,

(D.1)

where the differential operator
\hat{\Lambda}_{rig}^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}
is defined as

\hat{\Lambda}_{rig}^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}:=\Lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)M}\partial_{M},\qquad M=(\alpha\dot{\alpha},\alpha,\dot{\alpha},5),

(D.2)

with the parameters of the transformations as coefficients:

\Lambda^{(\alpha(s-2)\alpha)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\alpha})}=\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}-2i\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)-}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\alpha})}-2i\theta^{+(\alpha}\lambda^{\alpha(s-2))\dot{\alpha}(s-1)-}\,,

(D.3a)

\Lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)5}=\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)5}+2i\lambda^{(\alpha(s-2)\beta)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)-}\theta^{+}_{{\beta}}+2i\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\beta})-}\bar{\theta}^{+}_{{\dot{\beta}}}\,,

(D.3b)

\Lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)+}=\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)+}\,,\;\;\;\;\;\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\pm}=\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)i}u_{i}^{\pm}\,,

(D.3c)

\Lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)+}=\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)+}\,,\;\;\;\;\;\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)\pm}=\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)i}u_{i}^{\pm}\,.

(D.3d)

In (
D.2
) the same convention concerning the spinor indices inside the index
M
is assumed as in the differential operators (
5.33
).
All the parameters
\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}\,,\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)5}\,,

\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)i},\,\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)i}
in the operator
\hat{\Lambda}_{rig}^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}
are coordinate-independent.
The analytic-superspace localization of these parameters produces the spin
s
, spin
s-1
and
spin
s-\tfrac{1}{2}
gauge transformations, correspondingly.
The analytic operator (
D.2
)
satisfies the condition:

[\mathcal{D}^{++},\hat{\Lambda}_{rig}^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}]\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}=0\,.

(D.4)

This property is crucial for the invariance of the free hypermultiplet action (
5.1
) under (
D.1
).

Let us consider the algebra of these symmetry transformations. For simplicity, we shall limit our study to the massless case
(
i.e.
, with
\partial_{5}q^{+a}=0
).
The commutation relation of two rigid symmetries
D.1
has the following generic form:

\begin{split}[\delta^{(s)}_{\lambda_{1}},\delta^{(s)}_{{\lambda}_{2}}]q^{+a}=&\Lambda_{1}^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)N}\partial_{N}\Lambda_{2}^{\beta(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-2)M}\partial_{M}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\partial^{s-2}_{\beta(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-2)}\\
&-\Lambda_{2}^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)M}\partial_{M}\Lambda_{1}^{\beta(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-2)N}\partial_{N}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\partial^{s-2}_{\beta(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-2)}.\end{split}

(D.5)

Only transformations with fermionic parameters can give a nontrivial contribution to this commutator.
Keeping this in mind, we stick to the rigid spinor parameters
\lambda_{1}^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)i}
,

\lambda_{2}^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)i}
. A simple computation yields:

\begin{split}[\delta^{(s)}_{\lambda_{1}},\delta^{(s)}_{\lambda_{2}}]q^{+a}=&2i\left(\lambda_{1}^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)+}\lambda_{2}^{\beta(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-1)-}-\lambda_{1}^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)-}\lambda_{2}^{\beta(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-1)+}\right)\\
&\times\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\partial^{s-2}_{\beta(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-2)}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\beta}}q^{+a}+{\rm c.c.}\end{split}

(D.6)

Using the completeness relation

u^{+}_{i}u^{-}_{j}-u^{-}_{i}u^{+}_{j}=\epsilon_{ij}\,,

(D.7)

we obtain:

[\delta^{(s)}_{\lambda_{1}},\delta^{(s)}_{\lambda_{2}}]q^{+a}=2i\lambda_{1}^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)j}\left(\lambda_{2}^{\beta(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-1)}\right)_{j}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\partial^{s-2}_{\beta(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-2)}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\beta}}q^{+a}+{\rm c.c.}

(D.8)

For
s=2
case, we reproduce the standard result that commutator of two rigid supersymmetries
leads to translations.
In fact, the transformations (
D.1
) are closed only in the
s=2
case, while for
s>2

new transformations are
inevitably generated (as is expected for higher-spin algebras).

Let us adduce more details for the simplest non-trivial case of
s=3
:

[\delta^{(3)}_{\lambda_{1}},\delta^{(3)}_{\lambda_{2}}]q^{+a}=2i\lambda_{1}^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}j}\left(\lambda_{2}^{\gamma(\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma})}\right)_{j}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}q^{+a}+{\rm c.c.}

(D.9)

The transformation in the right hand side consists of the two irreducible pieces:

1. Totally symmetric one:

[\delta^{(3)}_{\lambda_{1}},\delta^{(3)}_{\lambda_{2}}]_{sym}q^{+a}=\lambda^{(\alpha\beta\gamma)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma})}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\gamma\dot{\gamma}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}q^{+a},

(D.10)

where:

\lambda^{(\alpha\beta\gamma)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma})}=2i\lambda_{1}^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}j}\left(\lambda_{2}^{\gamma(\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma})}\right)_{j}+{\rm symm}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)+{\rm c.c.}\,.

(D.11)

2. Antisymmetric in
[\alpha\gamma]
,
[\dot{\alpha}\dot{\gamma}]
:

[\delta^{(3)}_{\lambda_{1}},\delta^{(3)}_{\lambda_{2}}]_{antisym}q^{+a}=\lambda^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\Box q^{+a},

(D.12)

where:

\lambda^{\beta\dot{\beta}}=-i\left(\lambda_{1}\right)^{(\alpha\beta)j}_{\dot{\gamma}}\left(\lambda_{2}\right)^{(\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma})}_{\alpha j}\,+{\rm c.c.}

(D.13)

where we used the relation:

\partial_{[\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\gamma]\dot{\gamma}}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\alpha\gamma}\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\gamma}}\Box.

(D.14)

Thus the commutator of two rigid spin
\tfrac{3}{2}
supersymmetries generates a rigid bosonic spin
s=4
transformation associated with the
parameter
\lambda^{(\alpha\beta\gamma)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}\dot{\gamma})}
and
the spin
s=2
-like transformation with a vector parameter and one
\Box
operator acting on
q^{+a}
.
This clearly demonstrates that the algebra of transformations (
D.1
) is not closed for

s\geq 3

24
24

24

It is a crucial difference of the higher spin
3/2

supersymmetry (and its higher-rank cousins)
from those closed
3/2
superalgebras (and their higher-rank analogs) which were considered, e.g.,
in
32SUSY
.
.

In the general case, we see that the commutator of two symmetry transformations with spin
(s-\tfrac{3}{2})
parameters
results in a bosonic symmetry transformation with a spin
2s-3
parameter
(s\geq 2)
:

\lambda^{(\alpha(s-1)\beta(s-2))(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\beta}(s-1))}=2i\lambda_{1}^{(\alpha(s-1)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)j}\left(\lambda_{2}^{\beta(s-2))\dot{\beta}(s-1))}\right)_{j}+{\rm symm}(\alpha(s-1),\beta(s-2))+{\rm c.c.},

(D.15)

plus some extra parts (for
(s\geq 3)
) which include powers of
\Box
acting on
q^{+a}
and generalize those found in the
s=3
example.

As a simple generalization, one can also calculate the bracket of two different fermionic symmetries:

\begin{split}[\delta^{(s_{1})}_{\lambda_{1}},\delta^{(s_{2})}_{\lambda_{2}}]q^{+a}=&2i\left(\lambda_{1}^{\alpha(s_{1}-1)\dot{\alpha}(s_{1}-2)+}\lambda_{2}^{\beta(s_{2}-2)\dot{\beta}(s_{2}-1)-}-\lambda_{1}^{\alpha(s_{1}-1)\dot{\alpha}(s_{1}-2)-}\lambda_{2}^{\beta(s_{2}-2)\dot{\beta}(s_{2}-1)+}\right)\\
&\times\partial^{s_{1}-2}_{\alpha(s_{1}-2)\dot{\alpha}(s_{1}-2)}\partial^{s_{2}-2}_{\beta(s_{2}-2)\dot{\beta}(s_{2}-2)}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\beta}}\left(J\right)^{P(s_{1})+P(s_{2})}q^{+a}\\
=&2i\,\lambda_{1}^{\alpha(s_{1}-1)\dot{\alpha}(s_{1}-2)j}\left(\lambda_{2}^{\beta(s_{2}-2)\dot{\beta}(s_{2}-1)}\right)_{j}\\
&\times\partial^{s_{1}-2}_{\alpha(s_{1}-2)\dot{\alpha}(s_{1}-2)}\partial^{s_{2}-2}_{\beta(s_{2}-2)\dot{\beta}(s_{2}-2)}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\beta}}\left(J\right)^{P(s_{1}+s_{2})}q^{+a}.\end{split}

(D.16)

It leads to the bosonic rigid spin
s=s_{1}+s_{2}-2
symmetry transformation with the spin
s=s_{1}+s_{2}-3
constant parameter and some
additional symmetries involving powers of
\Box
acting on
q^{+a}
. For example, in the simplest nontrivial case (
s_{1}=2,\;s_{2}=3
)
the bracket of rigid spin
\tfrac{1}{2}

{\cal N}=2
supersymmetry with a rigid spin
\tfrac{3}{2}
supersymmetry in the right hand side yields, as a leading term,
the bosonic spin 3 symmetry transformation with the rank 2 symmetric traceless tensor constant parameter.

Here we considered the global symmetries of a free hypermultiplet. If we switch to the corresponding gauge transformations,
then a similar reasoning leads to the conclusion that in the right hand sides of the commutation relations,
gauge transformations of an increasing higher spins will appear. This implies
that in the hypothetical complete consistent interacting theory of
{\cal N}=2
higher spins, the entire infinite towers
of all higher spins should simultaneously participate, in agreement with a general belief.

D.2
Fermionic symmetry with spin
\tfrac{3}{2}
parameter

It is instructive to see how the above transformations look at the component level.
Let us consider the
s=3
rigid transformations.
It is easy to verify that the bosonic symmetry is the well-known spin
3
symmetry of the free bosonic action
Berends:1985xx
. We shall pay our attention to
the less trivial fermionic rigid symmetries:

\begin{split}\delta^{(3)}_{rig}q^{+a}=&\quad 2i\left(\lambda^{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}-}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\beta})}+\theta^{+(\alpha}\lambda^{\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})-}\right)\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}i(\tau_{3})^{a}_{\;b}q^{+b}\\
&-2i\left(\lambda^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}-}\theta^{+}_{\beta}+\lambda^{\alpha(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})-}\bar{\theta}^{+}_{\dot{\beta}}\right)\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{5}i(\tau_{3})^{a}_{\;b}q^{+b}\\
&+\left(\lambda^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}+}\partial^{-}_{\beta}+\lambda^{(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})\alpha+}\partial^{-}_{\dot{\alpha}}\right)\partial_{\alpha\dot{\beta}}i(\tau_{3})^{a}_{\;b}q^{+b}\,.\end{split}

(D.17)

They are characterized by the constant spin
\tfrac{3}{2}
Grassmann parameters
\lambda^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}i}
and
\bar{\lambda}^{(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})\alpha i}
. These transformations are realized on the on-shell hypermultiplet fields as:

\begin{cases}\delta f^{i}=i\lambda^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}i}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\chi_{\beta}+i\bar{\lambda}^{(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})\alpha i}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\psi}_{\dot{\beta}}\,,\\
\delta\bar{f}_{i}=-i\lambda_{i}^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\psi_{\beta}-i\bar{\lambda}_{i}^{(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})\alpha}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\chi}_{\dot{\beta}}\,,\\
\delta\psi_{\alpha}=-2\bar{\lambda}^{\beta(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})i}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{f}_{i}-2im\lambda_{\alpha}^{\beta\dot{\beta}i}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{f}_{i}\,,\\
\delta\bar{\psi}_{\dot{\alpha}}=2\lambda^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\beta}i}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}f_{i}-2im\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\alpha}}^{\beta\dot{\beta}i}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}f_{i}\,,\\
\delta\chi_{\alpha}=-2\bar{\lambda}^{\beta(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})i}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}f_{i}-2im\lambda_{\alpha}^{\beta\dot{\beta}i}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}f_{i}\,,\\
\delta\bar{\chi}_{\dot{\alpha}}=2\lambda^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\beta}i}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{f}_{i}-2im\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\alpha}}^{\dot{\beta}\beta i}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{f}_{i}\,.\end{cases}

(D.18)

The resulting transformations look similar to
\mathcal{N}=2
supersymmetry transformations
(
5.11
) of the free hypermultiplet, but involve more derivatives.
One can easily check that on-shell hypermultiplet action (
5.13
) is invariant under
these transformations (up to total derivatives).
So we observe that among symmetries of free hypermultiplet (
D.1
) there is a kind of

\mathcal{N}=2
“higher spin supersymmetry” transformations.
The corresponding conserved Noether current is given by the expression

\begin{split}J^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}_{(\mu\nu)\dot{\mu}i}=&\frac{i}{2}\left[\partial_{(\mu\dot{\mu}}\chi_{\nu)}\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{f}_{i}-\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{(\mu\dot{\mu}}\chi_{\nu)}\bar{f}_{i}\right]+\frac{i}{2}\left[\partial_{\mu\dot{\mu}}\psi_{\beta}\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}f_{i}-\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\mu\dot{\mu}}\psi_{\beta}f_{i}\right]\\
&-\delta_{(\mu}^{\alpha}\Big[\partial_{\nu)\dot{\mu}}\bar{f}_{i}\left(i\partial^{\dot{\alpha}\rho}\chi_{\rho}+m\bar{\psi}^{\dot{\alpha}}\right)+\partial_{\nu)\dot{\mu}}f_{i}\left(i\partial^{\dot{\alpha}\rho}\phi_{\rho}+m\bar{\chi}^{\dot{\alpha}}\right)\Big]\\
&+\delta^{\alpha}_{(\nu}\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\mu}}\Bigr[if_{i}\left(\Box\psi_{\mu}+m^{2}\psi_{\mu}\right)+i\bar{f}_{i}\left(\Box\chi_{\mu}+m^{2}\chi_{\mu}\right)\Bigr],\end{split}

(D.19)

\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}J^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}_{(\mu\nu)\dot{\mu}i}=0\,.

(D.20)

Note that the second and third lines of (
D.19
) are vanishing on the free hypermultiplet equations of motion (
5.12
).

One can calculate Lie bracket of such transformations, for example:

\begin{split}[\delta_{\lambda_{1}},\delta_{\bar{\lambda}_{2}}]f^{i}=&-2i\left(\lambda_{1}^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}j}\bar{\lambda}_{2}^{(\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma})\rho i}-\lambda_{1}^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}i}\bar{\lambda}_{2}^{(\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma})\rho j}\right)\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\sigma}}\partial_{\rho\dot{\rho}}f_{j}\\
=&-2i\lambda_{1}^{(\alpha\beta)\dot{\alpha}j}\left(\bar{\lambda}_{2}^{(\dot{\rho}\dot{\sigma})\rho}\right)_{j}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\sigma}}\partial_{\rho\dot{\rho}}f^{i}.\end{split}

(D.21)

So in the full agreement with the discussion of off-shell
s=3
transformation (
D.9
) in the previous Subsection
D.1
,
we observe that the commutator of two spin
\tfrac{3}{2}
“supersymmetries” yields spin
s=4
symmetry with a spin 3 parameter
and an extra
\Box
term.

D.3
Noether theorem

In this subsection we derive the current superfields corresponding to the rigid symmetries (
D.1
) of the free hypermultiplet action.

We will use Noether theorem in the form which can be simply extended to the superspace setting. If some continuous transformations with parameters
a^{A}
(
A
is an index enumerating symmetries)
form a rigid symmetry, then the variation of the action with the coordinate-dependent parameters
a^{A}(x)
has the universal form:

\delta S=\int d^{4}x\;\partial^{\mu}a^{A}(x)J^{A}_{\mu}(x)\,.

(D.22)

If
a^{A}(x)=const
we obtain that
\delta S=0
in agreement with the assumption that the given transformation is a symmetry of the action.
On the equation of motion, any field variation is vanishing:

\delta S=0\,,

(D.23)

and we conclude, that on equations of motions, due to an arbitrariness of the parameters
a^{A}(x)
, the current in (
D.22
) is subject
to the conservation law:

\partial^{\mu}J^{A}_{\mu}(x)=0\,.

(D.24)

Now we shall apply this general procedure to rigid symmetries of the free hypermultiplet. Varying the action (
5.1
) by the transformations (
D.1
) with arbitrary analytic parameters, which constitute a local version of the rigid symmetry
transformations from the previous subsection, we obtain:

\begin{split}\delta S=&\frac{1}{2}\int d\zeta^{(-4)}\;\Bigr[\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)M}\left(\partial_{M}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+a}\right)\mathcal{D}^{++}q^{+}_{a}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+q^{+a}\left(\mathcal{D}^{++}\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)M}\partial_{M}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\right)\Bigr].\end{split}

(D.25)

One can rewrite the second term using the relation:

\begin{split}\Bigr(\mathcal{D}^{++}\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)M}\partial_{M}&\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\Bigr)\\
=&\left(\mathcal{D}^{++}\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)M}\right)\partial_{M}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\\
&+\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)M}\left[\mathcal{D}^{++},\partial_{M}\right]\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\\
&+\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)M}\partial_{M}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}\mathcal{D}^{++}q^{+}_{a}\,.\end{split}

(D.26)

To obtain the conserved currents it is convenient to make use of the free equations of motion to eliminate terms involving
\mathcal{D}^{++}q^{+}_{a}
which vanish on the hypermultiplet mass shell.
It is also useful to separate, in the variation (
D.25
), the contributions with different index structures:

\delta S\Bigr|_{M=\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=\frac{1}{2}\int d\zeta^{(-4)}\;\left(\mathcal{D}^{++}\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}\right)q^{+a}\partial^{s-1}_{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\,,

(D.27)

\delta S\Bigr|_{M=5}=\frac{1}{2}\int d\zeta^{(-4)}\;\left(\mathcal{D}^{++}\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\right)q^{+a}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\partial_{5}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\,,

(D.28)

\begin{split}\delta S\Bigr|_{M=\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}\int d\zeta^{(-4)}\;&\left(\mathcal{D}^{++}\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)+}\right)q^{+a}\partial^{-}_{\alpha}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\\
&+\lambda^{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}q^{+a}[\mathcal{D}^{++},\partial^{-}_{\alpha}]\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\,,\end{split}

(D.29)

\begin{split}\delta S\Bigr|_{M=\dot{\alpha}}=\frac{1}{2}\int d\zeta^{(-4)}\;&\left(\mathcal{D}^{++}\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)+}\right)q^{+a}\partial^{-}_{\dot{\alpha}}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\\
&+\lambda^{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)+}q^{+a}[\mathcal{D}^{++},\partial^{-}_{\dot{\alpha}}]\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\,.\end{split}

(D.30)

One immediately observes that, if the parameters are rigid as in (
D.3
),
i.e.
, satisfy the condition
[\mathcal{D}^{++},\lambda^{M}\partial_{M}]=0
,
all these variations vanish in full agreement with the general statement of Noether theorem. In this way we derive the current superfield conservation laws:

\mathcal{D}^{++}\left(q^{+a}\partial^{s-1}_{\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\right)=0\,,

(D.31)

\mathcal{D}^{++}\left(q^{+a}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\partial_{5}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\right)=0\,,

(D.32)

\mathcal{D}^{++}\left(q^{+a}\partial^{-}_{\alpha}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\right)=q^{+a}[\mathcal{D}^{++},\partial^{-}_{\alpha}]\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\,,

(D.33)

\mathcal{D}^{++}\left(q^{+a}\partial^{-}_{\dot{\alpha}}\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\right)=q^{+a}[\mathcal{D}^{++},\partial^{-}_{\dot{\alpha}}]\partial^{s-2}_{\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}(J)^{P(s)}q^{+}_{a}\,.

(D.34)

The first two equations (
D.31
) and (
D.32
) directly give the laws of conservation of current superfields
(
5.46
) and (
5.49
).

Taking into account the equations

[\mathcal{D}^{++},\partial^{-}_{\alpha}]=-\partial^{+}_{\alpha}+2i\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+2i\theta^{+}_{\alpha}\partial_{5},

(D.35a)

[\mathcal{D}^{++},\partial^{-}_{\dot{\alpha}}]=-\partial^{+}_{\dot{\alpha}}-2i\theta^{+\alpha}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+2i\bar{\theta}^{+}_{\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{5},

(D.35b)

we can immediately identify eqs. (
D.33
) and (
D.34
) with the modified conservation laws (
5.55
) and (
5.62
).

So we conclude that the above equations fully reproduce the current superfield conservation conditions from Section
5.4

(we restricted our consideration to the
\xi=0
case, because
\xi\neq 0
gives a
trivial contribution to the current superfield) and give the explicit form of the current superfields. Thus we have established the direct connection between
the rigid symmetries (
D.1
) and the conserved current superfields. This Noether theorem can be considered as a generalization of Noether theorem in ordinary
superspace
Magro:2001aj
to the HSS case.

In full agreement with the results of Appendix
C.1
, the superfield conditions derived above
lead to the conservation of the bosonic spin
s
current, the doublet of fermionic spin
s-\tfrac{1}{2}
currents and the bosonic spin
s-1
current.
These currents correspond to the global spin
\mathbf{s}
symmetry (
D.1
) of the free hypermultiplet.

In conclusion of this Section, note that the conservation law (
5.82
)

\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}J^{++\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=0

(D.36)

does not directly follow from Noether theorem for rigid symmetries with the structure (
D.1
).
Presumably, it is related to some other symmetry of the free hypermultiplet. It would be interesting
to explicitly identify this extra bosonic symmetry.

Appendix E
Field redefinitions

In this Appendix we describe various field redefinitions capable to simplify the structure of the linearized cubic bosonic vertices in the spin 2 and spin 3 cases.

Before proceeding to concrete examples, it is important to note a simple observation that if a cubic vertex is equal to zero on free equations of motion,
then such a vertex can be removed by appropriate redefinition of fields. This observation is based on the property that, when redefining fields:

\phi\to\phi+F(\phi)\,,

(E.1)

the action is changing as

S[\phi]\to S[\phi]+\int d^{4}x\frac{\delta S[\phi]}{\delta\phi}F(\phi).

(E.2)

The variation of the action on the equations of motion is zero, which leads to the vanishing of the vertex on the equations of motion.
Such interactions are called "fake interactions", see e.g.
Ponomarev:2022vjb
.

E.1
Minimal gravity coupling of scalar fields and scalar field redefinitions

For comparing the results of Section
6.3
with those known in literature, it is more convenient to pass from the spinor notation for spin 2 fields to the more accustomed vector notation:

\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}\beta\dot{\beta}}:=\sigma^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}_{n}\sigma^{\beta\dot{\beta}}_{m}h^{(nm)}\,,

(E.3)

\mathcal{R}=\frac{1}{2}R=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial^{n}\partial^{m}-\eta^{nm}\Box\right)h_{mn}\,.

(E.4)

With this notation, the scalar part of on-shell hypermultiplet action (
5.13
) and the (2,0,0) coupling derived in (
6.24
) looks as:

\begin{split}S_{free}+S^{(s=2)}_{int}=&\int d^{4}x\,\left(\partial_{n}f^{i}\partial^{n}\bar{f}_{i}-m^{2}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)\\
&-\kappa_{2}\int d^{4}x\;h^{(mn)}\left(\partial_{m}\partial_{n}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}-2\partial_{m}f^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{f}_{i}+f^{i}\partial_{m}\partial_{n}\bar{f}_{i}\right)\\
&+\kappa_{2}\int d^{4}x\;R\left(f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\kappa_{2}^{2})\,.\end{split}

(E.5)

It will be convenient to integrate by parts in the second line to bring the interacting action in the form:

S^{(s=2)}_{int}=\kappa_{2}\int d^{4}x\;\left(4h^{(mn)}\partial_{m}f^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{f}_{i}-\Box hf^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\kappa_{2}^{2}).

(E.6)

On the other hand, the standard (non-linear) minimal
(2,0,0)
interaction has a different form:

S^{(s=2)}_{min}=\int d^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\;\left(g^{mn}\partial_{m}f^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{f}_{i}-m^{2}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right).

(E.7)

To compare it with our result, we need to consider the linearized cubic vertices that follow from this Lagrangian.
To do this, it is necessary to establish a relationship between the metric
g_{mn}
and the fields (
E.3
) used for spin 2.
According to
Galperin:1987ek
;
Galperin:1987em
the full analytic
\mathcal{N}=2
supergravity potential
H^{++m}
in Wess-Zumino gauge has the form:

H^{++m}=-2i\theta^{+}\sigma^{a}\bar{\theta}^{+}e_{a}^{m}+\dots,

(E.8)

where
e_{a}^{m}
is the “inverse vierbein”, i.e.:

g_{mn}=\eta_{ab}e^{a}_{m}e^{b}_{n}.

(E.9)

Using the relation

H^{++m}=-2i\theta^{+}\sigma^{m}\bar{\theta}^{+}+\kappa_{2}h^{++\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\sigma^{m}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}},

(E.10)

the spin
\mathbf{2}
Wess-Zumino gauge (
3.13
) and the relation (
E.3
), we obtain:

e_{a}^{m}=\delta_{a}^{m}+2\kappa_{2}h_{a}^{m}.

(E.11)

So we have:

g_{mn}=\eta_{mn}-4\kappa_{2}h_{mn}+\mathcal{O}(h^{2})\,,\qquad\sqrt{-g}=1-2\kappa_{2}h+\mathcal{O}(\kappa_{2}^{2})\,,

(E.12)

and obtain that the action (
E.7
), to the linear order in the spin 2 field
h^{mn}
, reads:

\begin{split}S^{(s=2)}_{int-min}=&\int d^{4}x\;\left(\eta^{mn}\partial_{m}f^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{f}_{i}-m^{2}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)\\
&+4\kappa_{2}\int d^{4}x\;h^{mn}\partial_{m}f^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{f}_{i}-2\kappa_{2}\int d^{4}x\;h\left(\eta^{mn}\partial_{m}f^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{f}_{i}-m^{2}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\kappa_{2}^{2})\,.\end{split}

(E.13)

Now we redefine the scalar field as

f^{i}=\left(-g\right)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathfrak{f}^{i}=\mathfrak{f}^{i}+\kappa_{2}h\mathfrak{f}^{i}+\mathcal{O}(\kappa_{2}^{2})\,.

(E.14)

Then the free kinetic term of
f^{i}
is transformed to

\begin{split}S_{free}=&\int d^{4}x\;\left(\eta^{mn}\partial_{m}f^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{f}_{i}-m^{2}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)\\
=&\int d^{4}x\;\left(\eta^{mn}\partial_{m}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-m^{2}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right)\\
&+2\kappa_{2}\int d^{4}x\;h\left(\eta^{mn}\partial_{m}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-m^{2}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right)-\kappa_{2}\int d^{4}x\;\Box h\left(\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\kappa_{2}^{2})\,,\end{split}

(E.15)

which modifies the linearized cubic interaction. The action (
E.13
) takes the form:

\begin{split}S^{(s=2)}_{int-min}=&\int d^{4}x\;\left(\eta^{mn}\partial_{m}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-m^{2}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right)\\
&+4\kappa_{2}\int d^{4}x\;h^{mn}\partial_{m}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial_{n}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-\kappa_{2}\int d^{4}x\;\Box h\left(\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\kappa_{2}^{2})\,.\end{split}

(E.16)

Thus the interaction of spin 2 with scalars (
E.6
) found in Section
6.3
, actually coincides with the minimal (
E.7
)
up to the field redefinitions (
E.14
).

E.2
Redefinition of the spin 2 gauge field and
(2,0,0)
coupling

In this Subsection we study how a redefinition of the spin 2 gauge field can affect the structure of the (2, 0, 0) vertex.

Recall the Pauli-Fierz action (
A.20
) in the spinor notations:

\begin{split}S_{PF}(\Phi^{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})},\Upsilon)&=-\int d^{4}x\;\Big[\Phi^{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})}\Box\Phi_{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})}-\Phi^{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial^{\rho\dot{\rho}}\Phi_{(\rho\beta)(\dot{\rho}\dot{\beta})}\\
&\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;+2\,\Phi\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\Phi_{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})}-6\Phi\Box\Phi\Big].\end{split}

(E.17)

Redefining the field
\Phi
,

\Phi\,\Rightarrow\,\Phi+af^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\,,

(E.18)

leads to:

\begin{split}S_{PF}(\Phi^{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})},\Phi)\,\Rightarrow\,&S_{PF}(\Phi^{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})},\Phi)\\
&-a\int d^{4}x\;\left[2\Phi^{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\left(f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)-12\Phi\Box\left(f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)\right]+\mathcal{O}(a^{2}).\end{split}

(E.19)

After integration by parts one can rewrite the second line in terms of scalar curvature
\mathcal{R}
introduced in (
A.11
):

S_{PF}(\Phi^{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})},\Phi)\;\Rightarrow\;S_{PF}(\Phi^{(\alpha\beta)(\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta})},\Phi)-16a\int d^{4}x\;\mathcal{R}\left(f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(a^{2})\,.

(E.20)

Thus we have shown that at the linearized level, the
(2,0,0)
vertex of form
\mathcal{R}\bar{f}f
can be eliminated
by the redefinition (
E.18
) of the spin 2 field trace. This property was used, e.g., in the discussion in the end of Section
6.4
.

E.3
Redefinition of scalar fields and
(3,0,0)
coupling

1.
In a direct analogy with (
E.14
) it is possible to redefine the complex scalar fields as:

f^{i}=\mathfrak{f}^{i}-ib\left(\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\right)\mathfrak{f}^{i}\,,\qquad\bar{f}_{i}=\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}+ib\left(\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\right)\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\,.

(E.21)

Here
b
is some real parameter and the field
\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}
was defined in (
A.29
). Under this redefinition the free scalar Lagrangian changes as:

S_{free}(f^{i})=S_{free}(\mathfrak{f}^{i})-\frac{ib}{2}\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\left(\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-\partial^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(b^{2})\,.

(E.22)

So, making the redefinition (
E.21
), one can eliminate the cubic vertex of the form appearing, e.g., in (
6.53
).

2.
There is one more admissible scalar fields redefinition:

f^{i}=\mathfrak{f}^{i}-ic\,\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\,,\qquad\bar{f}_{i}=\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}+ic\,\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\,.

(E.23)

This redefinition leads to:

\begin{split}S_{free}(f^{i})=&\;S_{free}(\mathfrak{f}^{i})-\frac{ic}{2}\Phi^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\left(\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right)\\
&+ic\;m^{2}\Phi^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\left(\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(c^{2}).\end{split}

(E.24)

Further manipulations lead to the expression:

\begin{split}S_{free}(f^{i})=&\;S_{free}(\mathfrak{f}^{i})-\frac{ic}{2}\int d^{4}x\,\Phi^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\Box\left(\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right)\\
&+\frac{ic}{2}\;\int d^{4}x\,\Phi^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\Bigr\{\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}(\Box+m^{2})\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}+\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\mathfrak{f}^{i}(\Box+m^{2})\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(\Box+m^{2})\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}(\Box+m^{2})\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\Bigr\}+\mathcal{O}(c^{2}).\end{split}

(E.25)

So on the free equations of motions for
\mathfrak{f}^{i}
one can eliminate the cubic
(3,0,0)
coupling of the form:

\int d^{4}x\,\Phi^{\beta\dot{\beta}}\,\Box\,\left(\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\mathfrak{f}^{i}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}-\mathfrak{f}^{i}\partial_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\bar{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}\right).

(E.26)

E.4
Redefinition of spin 3 gauge field and
(3,0,0)
coupling

The spin 3 action (
A.42
) has the form:

\displaystyle S^{(s=3)}\Big(\Phi^{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})},\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Big)=\int d^{4}x\,\Big[\Phi^{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})}\Box\Phi_{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})}

\displaystyle-\,\frac{3}{2}\Phi^{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})}\partial_{\alpha_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{1}}\partial^{\rho\dot{\rho}}\Phi_{(\rho\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\rho}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})}+3\Phi^{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})}\partial_{\alpha_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{1}}\partial_{\alpha_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{2}}\Phi_{\alpha_{3}\dot{\alpha}_{3}}

\displaystyle\,-\frac{15}{4}\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Box\Phi_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+\frac{3}{8}\partial_{\alpha_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{1}}\Phi^{\alpha_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{1}}\partial_{\alpha_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{2}}\Phi^{\alpha_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{2}}\Big].

(E.27)

After the field redefinition,

\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Rightarrow\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+iu\left(f^{i}\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{f}_{i}-\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right),

(E.28)

the action is redefined as:

\begin{split}S^{(s=3)}\Big(&\Phi^{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})},\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Big)\Rightarrow\,S^{(s=3)}\Big(\Phi^{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})},\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Big)\\
&+iu\int d^{4}x\;\Bigr[3\Phi^{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})}\partial_{\alpha_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{1}}\partial_{\alpha_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{2}}\left(f^{i}\partial_{\alpha_{3}\dot{\alpha}_{3}}\bar{f}_{i}-\partial_{\alpha_{3}\dot{\alpha}_{3}}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{15}{2}\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Box\left(f^{i}\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{f}_{i}-\partial^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{3}{4}\Phi^{\alpha_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{1}}\partial_{\alpha_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{1}}\partial_{\alpha_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{2}}\left(f^{i}\partial^{\alpha_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{2}}\bar{f}_{i}-\partial^{\alpha_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{2}}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)\Bigr]+\mathcal{O}(u^{2})\,.\end{split}

(E.29)

Using the definition of the linearized spin 3 curvature (
A.39
) and integrating by parts, one can rewrite the transformation (
E.29
) as

\begin{split}S^{(s=3)}\Big(&\Phi^{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})},\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Big)\;\Rightarrow\;S^{(s=3)}\Big(\Phi^{(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})(\dot{\alpha}_{1}\dot{\alpha}_{2}\dot{\alpha}_{3})},\Phi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\Big)\\
&+iu\int d^{4}x\;\Bigr[3\mathcal{R}^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\left(f^{i}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{f}_{i}-\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right)\Bigr]+\mathcal{O}(u^{2})\,.\end{split}

(E.30)

So, the field redefinition (
E.28
) ensures eliminating the
(3,0,0)
cubic couplings of the form:

i\int d^{4}x\;\mathcal{R}^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\left(f^{i}\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\bar{f}_{i}-\partial_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}f^{i}\bar{f}_{i}\right).

(E.31)

Appendix F
Gauge-fixing

The aim of this Section is to rewrite the superfield equations of motions for gauge superfields in terms of unconstrained analytic potentials
h^{++\dots}
subject to the proper manifestly
supersymmetric gauge conditions. Equations (
4.43
) are written in terms of
the superfields
G^{--\dots}
defined through the zero curvature equations (
3.4
), (
3.5
) and related to
h^{++\dots}
by eqs. (
3.2
) and (
3.3
).
It would be very convenient to write the equations directly through the analytic gauge potentials.

F.1
Spin
\mathbf{1}
theory

\mathcal{N}=2
Maxwell multiplet is described by the analytic superfield
h^{++}(\zeta)
with the gauge transformation:

\delta h^{++}=\mathcal{D}^{++}\lambda\,.

(F.1)

and the equation of motion

(D^{+})^{4}h^{--}=0\,,

(F.2)

where
h^{--}
is a solution of the zero-curvature equation,

\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{--}=\mathcal{D}^{--}h^{++}\,.

(F.3)

Let us consider the superfield transformation law of
\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++}
:

\delta\left(\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++}\right)=\mathcal{D}^{++}\mathcal{D}^{++}\lambda\,.

(F.4)

This law prompts that, in addition to the Wess-Zumino gauge
(
4.4
), one can gauge away one more component from

\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++}
. Indeed, keeping in mind that

\left(\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++}\right)_{gauge}=-2(\theta^{+})^{2}(\bar{\theta}^{+})^{2}\partial_{m}A^{m}\,,

(F.5)

and making use of the residual gauge freedom,

\lambda(\zeta)=a(x)\,,

(F.6)

we can choose Lorentz gauge,

\delta\left(\partial_{m}A^{m}\right)=\Box a\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\partial_{m}A^{m}=0\,,

(F.7)

that further restricts the residual gauge freedom to

\lambda(\zeta)=a(x)\,,\qquad\Box a(x)=0\,.

(F.8)

Gauge-fixing
\Big(\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++}\Big)_{gauge}=0
is just the
\mathcal{N}=2
superfield generalization of Lorentz gauge in Maxwell theory.

It is possible from the very beginning to fix the gauge in the manifestly supersymmetric way as

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++}=0\;\;\;\;\;&\Rightarrow\;\;\;\;\;h^{++}=h^{(ij)}(Z)u^{+}_{i}u^{+}_{j}\;\;\;\;\;\\
&\Rightarrow\;\;\;\;\;h^{--}=h^{(ij)}(Z)u^{-}_{i}u^{-}_{j}=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}^{--}\mathcal{D}^{--}h^{++}\,.\end{split}

(F.9)

Here we used the cental basis in HSS and, at the last step, rewrote the result of solving the harmonic zero-curvature condition in the covariant basis-independent form.

It is straightforward to check that, taking into account the analyticity of
h^{++}
, we can transform the equation of motion (
F.2
) to the form

(D^{+})^{4}h^{--}=\frac{1}{2}(D^{+})^{4}(D^{--})^{2}h^{++}\sim\Box h^{++}\,.

(F.10)

So we conclude that
\mathcal{N}=2
superspin
1
can be described by a pair of equations:

\begin{cases}\Box h^{++}=0\,,\\
\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++}=0\,.\end{cases}

(F.11)

The first equation is the massless Klein-Gordon equation, the second one is the gauge-fixing condition.

F.2
Spin
\mathbf{2}
theory

The spin
\mathbf{2}
equations (
4.46
) are more complicated:

\begin{cases}(D^{+})^{4}G^{--\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=0\,,\\
(D^{+})^{4}G^{--5}=0\,,\\
(\bar{D}^{+})^{2}D^{+\alpha}G^{--}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}-(D^{+})^{2}\bar{D}^{+}_{\dot{\alpha}}G^{--5}=0\,,\\
(D^{+})^{2}\bar{D}^{+\dot{\alpha}}G^{--}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+(\bar{D}^{+})^{2}D^{+}_{\alpha}G^{--5}=0\,.\end{cases}

(F.12)

It is convenient to choose the covariant gauge in full analogy to the spin
\mathbf{1}
case:

\mathcal{D}^{++}G^{++\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=0\,,

(F.13a)

\mathcal{D}^{++}G^{++5}=0\,.

(F.13b)

Note, that this gauge-fixing condition explicitly involves the spinor coordinates
\theta^{+\hat{\alpha}}
. In terms of the analytic potentials these conditions amount to the set:

\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++\alpha+}=0\,,\qquad\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++\dot{\alpha}+}=0\,,

(F.14a)

\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++\alpha\dot{\alpha}}+2ih^{++\alpha+}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\alpha}}-2ih^{++\dot{\alpha}+}\theta^{+\alpha}=0\,,

(F.14b)

\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++5}-2ih^{++\alpha+}\theta^{+}_{\alpha}-2ih^{++\dot{\alpha}+}\bar{\theta}^{+}_{\dot{\alpha}}=0\,.

(F.14c)

This gauge is just the superfield form of de-Donder gauge:

\partial_{m}\bar{h}^{mn}=0\,,\qquad\text{where}\qquad\bar{h}^{mn}:=h^{mn}-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{mn}h\,.

(F.15)

In this gauge, one can resolve the zero-curvature conditions in analogy with (
F.9
):

G^{--\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{D}^{--})^{2}G^{++\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\,,

(F.16)

G^{--5}=\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{D}^{--})^{2}G^{++5}\,.

(F.17)

Using these solutions, one arrives at the equations of motions written in terms of the analytic potentials only:

\begin{cases}\Box h^{++\alpha\dot{\alpha}}-4\partial^{\mu(\dot{\alpha}}\partial^{-}_{\mu}h^{++\alpha\dot{)}+}+4\partial^{(\alpha\dot{\mu}}\bar{\partial}^{-}_{\dot{\mu}}h^{++\dot{\alpha})+}=0,\\
\Box h^{++\alpha+}=0,\\
\Box h^{++\dot{\alpha}+}=0\,,\\
\Box h^{++5}+2\partial^{\mu\dot{\mu}}\partial^{-}_{\dot{\mu}}h^{+++}_{\mu}-2\partial^{\mu\dot{\mu}}\partial^{-}_{\mu}h^{+++}_{\dot{\mu}}=0\,.\end{cases}

(F.18)

We see that, in addition to the
\Box
terms, the first and fourth equations contain additional terms linear in the derivatives.
The first equation, in particular, yields:

\Box\bar{h}^{mn}=0\,.

(F.19)

Thus the equations of motion for the spin
{\bf 2}
case amount to the Lorentz-type covariant gauge conditions (
F.14a
) - (
F.14c
)
and eqs. (
F.18
), all written in terms of analytic potentials.

F.3
General spin
\mathbf{s}
theory

One can easily repeat the same steps as in spin
\mathbf{2}
case for the generic spin
{\bf s}
superfield equation (
4.43
):

(D^{+})^{4}G^{--\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}=0\,.

(F.20)

It is possible to show that this equation is equivalent to the system of equations:

\begin{cases}\Box G^{++\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}+i\partial^{\mu\dot{\mu}}\left(D^{-}_{\mu}\bar{D}^{+}_{\dot{\mu}}-\bar{D}^{-}_{\dot{\mu}}D^{+}_{\mu}\right)G^{++\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}=0\,,\\
D^{++}G^{++\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}=0\,.\end{cases}

(F.21)

Using the relation (
3.2
), this system can be represented as a system of equations for the analytic potentials.

The gauge conditions in terms of the analytic potentials read:

\begin{cases}D^{++}h^{++\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}+2i\big[h^{++\alpha(s-1)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)+}\bar{\theta}^{+\dot{\alpha}_{s-1})}+h^{++\dot{\alpha}(s-1)(\alpha(s-2)+}\,\theta^{+\alpha_{s-1})}\big]=0\,,\\
D^{++}h^{++\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)+}=0\,,\\
D^{++}h^{++\dot{\alpha}(s-1)\alpha(s-2)+}=0\,.\end{cases}

(F.22)

The superfield equations of motion are written as

\begin{cases}\Box h^{++\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}-4\partial^{\mu(\dot{\alpha}(s-1)}\partial^{-}_{\mu}h^{++\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2))+}+4\partial^{(\alpha(s-1)\dot{\mu}}\bar{\partial}^{-}_{\dot{\mu}}h^{++\dot{\alpha}(s-1)\alpha(s-2))+}=0\,,\\
\Box h^{++\alpha(s-1)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)+}=0\,,\\
\Box h^{++\dot{\alpha}(s-1)\alpha(s-2)+}=0\,.\end{cases}

(F.23)

The equation

(D^{+})^{4}G^{--5\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}=0

(F.24)

can also be restructured in the same way and it amounts to the gauge condition

\mathcal{D}^{++}h^{++5\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}-2ih^{++(\alpha(s-2)\rho)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}\theta^{+}_{\rho}-2ih^{++\alpha(s-2)(\dot{\alpha}(s-2)\dot{\rho})}\bar{\theta}^{+}_{\dot{\rho}}=0

(F.25)

and the following equation for the analytic potentials:

\Box h^{++5\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)}+2\partial^{\mu\dot{\mu}}\partial^{-}_{\dot{\mu}}h^{++\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)+}_{\mu}-2\partial^{\mu\dot{\mu}}\partial^{-}_{\mu}h^{++\alpha(s-2)\dot{\alpha}(s-2)+}_{\dot{\mu}}=0\,.

(F.26)

The results presented in this Appendix should be considered as first steps in the study of representations of
\mathcal{N}=2
supersymmetry algebra
on harmonic superfields from the group-theory point of view. More complete analysis of the group-theoretical origin of these equations will
be a subject of separate publication.
